+#!/usr/bin/env python3
+"""Compare puzzle solution lengths without validating moves."""
+
+from __future__ import annotations
+
+import argparse
+import csv
+import glob
+import re
+from dataclasses import dataclass
+from pathlib import Path
+
+
+@dataclass(frozen=True)
+class Solution:
+    source: str
+    text: str
+
+    @property
+    def length(self) -> int:
+        if not self.text:
+            return 0
+        return len([move for move in self.text.split(".") if move])
+
+
+def read_submission_csv(path: Path, source: str) -> dict[int, Solution]:
+    rows: dict[int, Solution] = {}
+    with path.open(newline="", encoding="utf-8-sig") as fh:
+        reader = csv.DictReader(fh)
+        if not reader.fieldnames:
+            return rows
+        id_field = "initial_state_id" if "initial_state_id" in reader.fieldnames else reader.fieldnames[0]
+        solution_field = "path" if "path" in reader.fieldnames else reader.fieldnames[-1]
+        for row in reader:
+            raw_id = (row.get(id_field) or "").strip()
+            solution = (row.get(solution_field) or "").strip()
+            if not raw_id:
+                continue
+            rows[int(raw_id)] = Solution(source, solution)
+    return rows
+
+
+def read_solve_reflect_logs(log_dir: Path) -> dict[int, Solution]:
+    rows: dict[int, Solution] = {}
+    for path_text in sorted(glob.glob(str(log_dir / "slurm-*.out"))):
+        path = Path(path_text)
+        text = path.read_text(encoding="utf-8", errors="ignore")
+        puzzle_ids = re.findall(r"original_puzzle_id=(\d+)", text)
+        if not puzzle_ids:
+            continue
+        puzzle_id = int(puzzle_ids[-1])
+
+        candidates: list[Solution] = []
+        original_solutions = re.findall(r"original_solution=([^\s]+)", text)
+        if original_solutions:
+            candidates.append(Solution(f"{path.name}:original", original_solutions[-1]))
<truncated omitted_approx_tokens="293" />ngth if old else None
+        new_len = new.length if new else None
+        delta = None if old_len is None or new_len is None else new_len - old_len
+        if delta is not None:
+            total_delta += delta
+            if delta < 0:
+                improved += 1
+            elif delta > 0:
+                worsened += 1
+            else:
+                equal += 1
+        lines.append(
+            "| {puzzle_id} | {old_len} | {new_len} | {delta} | {source} | `{solution}` |".format(
+                puzzle_id=puzzle_id,
+                old_len="" if old_len is None else old_len,
+                new_len="" if new_len is None else new_len,
+                delta="" if delta is None else delta,
+                source="" if new is None else new.source,
+                solution="" if new is None else new.text,
+            )
+        )
+
+    lines.append("")
+    lines.append(
+        f"Итого: delta={total_delta}, improved={improved}, equal={equal}, worsened={worsened}"
+    )
+    return "\n".join(lines)
+
+
+def main() -> int:
+    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(
+        description="Compare solution lengths only; no puzzle validation is performed."
+    )
+    parser.add_argument("--baseline-csv", required=True, type=Path)
+    source = parser.add_mutually_exclusive_group(required=True)
+    source.add_argument("--candidate-csv", type=Path)
+    source.add_argument("--logs-dir", type=Path)
+    parser.add_argument("--start", type=int)
+    parser.add_argument("--end", type=int)
+    args = parser.parse_args()
+
+    baseline = read_submission_csv(args.baseline_csv, "baseline")
+    if args.candidate_csv:
+        candidate = read_submission_csv(args.candidate_csv, args.candidate_csv.name)
+    else:
+        candidate = read_solve_reflect_logs(args.logs_dir)
+
+    print(format_markdown(baseline, candidate, args.start, args.end))
+    return 0
+
+
+if __name__ == "__main__":
+    raise SystemExit(main())

The T4 notebook builds `sm_75`, uses the multi-node rendezvous torchrun path with `TORCHRUN_NNODES=1` and `TORCHRUN_NPROC_PER_NODE=2`, exports the provided `repo_output1` model path when present, runs puzzle `992` at `BEAM_WIDTH=2**24` and `DEPTH_LIMIT=80`, and passes the requested static-hybrid/manual shard/Stream1/Stream3/Stream4 config through environment variables. Added `kaggle_t4_torchrun/` as a separate Kaggle push package for slug `trydotatwo/cayley-beam-gpu-t4-torchrun`. Ve<truncated omitted_approx_tokens="1746" />d with manually spawned rank processes.
- Changed the MEPhI torchrun launcher logging to match the Kaggle notebook's readable-rank behavior. Torchrun now launches a small shell wrapper: rank 0 writes to the main `tee`/SLURM log, while ranks 1..N write themselves to `${LOG_DIR}/ranks_${SLURM_JOB_ID:-manual}/rank${RANK}.log`, avoiding interleaved multi-rank depth lines in the main output.
- Added a MEPhI 8xA100 tuning launcher set. `tune_8xa100_stream1.sh` builds/runs `stream_benchmark` and writes `logs/best_stream1.env`; `tune_8xa100_pipeline.sh` runs 8-rank torchrun production sweeps to depth 12 across shard count, Stream3 ring slots, Stream4 batch, and trigger size, records pass/fail plus average full-depth runtime in TSV, and writes `logs/best_pipeline.env`; `start_8xa100_best.sh` sources that best config for the final production run. Shared MEPhI preflight/build/torchrun/capacity logic now lives in `hpc/mephi_8xa100_common.sh`.
- Fixed MEPhI tuning launcher issues found on `basis`: SLURM spools submitted scripts under `/var/lib/slurm/slurmd/job...`, so the tuning scripts now source `mephi_8xa100_common.sh` from `${SLURM_SUBMIT_DIR:-$(pwd)}` instead of `BASH_SOURCE[0]`; `tune_8xa100_stream1.sh` now parses `b_micro=` from `stream_benchmark` output when writing `logs/best_stream1.env`.
- Fixed the MEPhI pipeline sweep average-depth parser (`printf "%.6f", sum / n`) and added automatic per-config GPU utilization logging. `beam_torchrun_production` now runs a background `nvidia-smi` sampler every 5 seconds while torchrun is active and writes `logs/tuning_<job>/nvidia_smi_<run_tag>.log`, then stops the sampler after the config exits.
- Hardened MEPhI pipeline history cleanup: the sweep now clears history through `beam_safe_clear_history_contents`, which requires `realpath(HISTORY_DIR) == realpath(JOB_DIR)/history` and removes only first-level entries inside that directory. This replaces the direct `rm -rf "${HISTORY_DIR:?}/"*` sweep cleanup.

git fetch origin main --depth 1

cd /mnt/pool/6/vokirova/beam8a100

cp repo/hpc/tune_8xa100_staged.sh .
sed -i 's/\r$//' mephi_8xa100_common.sh tune_8xa100_staged.sh
bash -n mephi_8xa100_common.sh tune_8xa100_staged.sh
chmod +x tune_8xa100_staged.sh

sbatch -p kaf12 tune_8xa100_staged.sh

+#!/usr/bin/env python3
+"""Compare puzzle solution lengths without validating moves."""
+
+from __future__ import annotations
+
+import argparse
+import csv
+import glob
+import re
+from dataclasses import dataclass
+from pathlib import Path
+
+
+@dataclass(frozen=True)
+class Solution:
+    source: str
+    text: str
+
+    @property
+    def length(self) -> int:
+        if not self.text:
+            return 0
+        return len([move for move in self.text.split(".") if move])
+
+
+def read_submission_csv(path: Path, source: str) -> dict[int, Solution]:
+    rows: dict[int, Solution] = {}
+    with path.open(newline="", encoding="utf-8-sig") as fh:
+        reader = csv.DictReader(fh)
+        if not reader.fieldnames:
+            return rows
+        id_field = "initial_state_id" if "initial_state_id" in reader.fieldnames else reader.fieldnames[0]
+        solution_field = "path" if "path" in reader.fieldnames else reader.fieldnames[-1]
+        for row in reader:
+            raw_id = (row.get(id_field) or "").strip()
+            solution = (row.get(solution_field) or "").strip()
+            if not raw_id:
+                continue
+            rows[int(raw_id)] = Solution(source, solution)
+    return rows
+
+
+def read_solve_reflect_logs(log_dir: Path) -> dict[int, Solution]:
+    rows: dict[int, Solution] = {}
+    for path_text in sorted(glob.glob(str(log_dir / "slurm-*.out"))):
+        path = Path(path_text)
+        text = path.read_text(encoding="utf-8", errors="ignore")
+        puzzle_ids = re.findall(r"original_puzzle_id=(\d+)", text)
+        if not puzzle_ids:
+            continue
+        puzzle_id = int(puzzle_ids[-1])
+
+        candidates: list[Solution] = []
+        original_solutions = re.findall(r"original_solution=([^\s]+)", text)
+        if original_solutions:
+            candidates.append(Solution(f"{path.name}:original", original_solutions[-1]))
<truncated omitted_approx_tokens="293" />ngth if old else None
+        new_len = new.length if new else None
+        delta = None if old_len is None or new_len is None else new_len - old_len
+        if delta is not None:
+            total_delta += delta
+            if delta < 0:
+                improved += 1
+            elif delta > 0:
+                worsened += 1
+            else:
+                equal += 1
+        lines.append(
+            "| {puzzle_id} | {old_len} | {new_len} | {delta} | {source} | `{solution}` |".format(
+                puzzle_id=puzzle_id,
+                old_len="" if old_len is None else old_len,
+                new_len="" if new_len is None else new_len,
+                delta="" if delta is None else delta,
+                source="" if new is None else new.source,
+                solution="" if new is None else new.text,
+            )
+        )
+
+    lines.append("")
+    lines.append(
+        f"Итого: delta={total_delta}, improved={improved}, equal={equal}, worsened={worsened}"
+    )
+    return "\n".join(lines)
+
+
+def main() -> int:
+    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(
+        description="Compare solution lengths only; no puzzle validation is performed."
+    )
+    parser.add_argument("--baseline-csv", required=True, type=Path)
+    source = parser.add_mutually_exclusive_group(required=True)
+    source.add_argument("--candidate-csv", type=Path)
+    source.add_argument("--logs-dir", type=Path)
+    parser.add_argument("--start", type=int)
+    parser.add_argument("--end", type=int)
+    args = parser.parse_args()
+
+    baseline = read_submission_csv(args.baseline_csv, "baseline")
+    if args.candidate_csv:
+        candidate = read_submission_csv(args.candidate_csv, args.candidate_csv.name)
+    else:
+        candidate = read_solve_reflect_logs(args.logs_dir)
+
+    print(format_markdown(baseline, candidate, args.start, args.end))
+    return 0
+
+
+if __name__ == "__main__":
+    raise SystemExit(main())

Torchrun now launches a small shell wrapper: rank 0 writes to the main `tee`/SLURM log, while ranks 1..N write themselves to `${LOG_DIR}/ranks_${SLURM_JOB_ID:-manual}/rank${RANK}.log`, avoiding interleaved multi-rank depth lines in the main output.

- Fixed the MEPhI pipeline sweep average-depth parser (`printf "%.6f", sum / n`) and added automatic per-config GPU utilization logging. `beam_torchrun_production` now runs a background `nvidia-smi` sampler every 5 seconds while torchrun is active and writes `logs/tuning_<job>/nvidia_smi_<run_tag>.log`, then stops the sampler after the config exits.
- Hardened MEPhI pipeline history cleanup: the sweep now clears history through `beam_safe_clear_history_contents`, which requires `realpath(HISTORY_DIR) == realpath(JOB_DIR)/history` and removes only first-level entries inside that directory. This replaces the direct `rm -rf "${HISTORY_DIR:?}/"*` sweep cleanup.

 #include <cuda_runtime.h>
+#include <cuda_bf16.h>
 #include <cuda_fp16.h>
@@
 __device__ std::uint32_t score_key_from_float_device(float q) {
@@
 }
+
+__device__ float stream1_load_weight_device(const half* ptr, std::uint64_t idx, std::uint32_t dtype) {
+    if (dtype == STREAM1_DTYPE_BF16) {
+#if defined(__CUDA_ARCH__) && __CUDA_ARCH__ >= 800
+        return __bfloat162float(reinterpret_cast<const __nv_bfloat16*>(ptr)[idx]);
+#else
+        return __half2float(ptr[idx]);
+#endif
+    }
+    return __half2float(ptr[idx]);
+}
+
+__device__ void stream1_layer_norm_relu_device(
+    float* values,
+    const half* gamma,
+    const half* beta,
+    std::uint32_t cols,
+    std::uint32_t dtype) {
+    __shared__ float reduce[256];
+    float local_sum = 0.0f;
+    for (std::uint32_t i = threadIdx.x; i < cols; i += blockDim.x) {

+inline void alloc_managed_pointer_table(half**& table, const std::vector<half*>& ptrs, const char* name) {
+    if (ptrs.empty()) {
+        table = nullptr;
+        return;
+    }
+    BEAM_CUDA_CHECK(cudaMallocManaged(&table, ptrs.size() * sizeof(half*)));
+    for (std::size_t i = 0; i < ptrs.size(); ++i) {
+        table[i] = ptrs[i];
+    }
+    BEAM_CUDA_CHECK(cudaDeviceSynchronize());
+    require_aligned(table, alignof(half*), name);
+}

 inline void alloc_and_copy(half*& dst, const std::vector<std::byte>& src, const char* name) {

+
+inline void alloc_managed_pointer_table(half**& table, const std::vector<half*>& ptrs, const char* name) {
+    if (ptrs.empty()) {
+        table = nullptr;
+        return;
+    }
+    BEAM_CUDA_CHECK(cudaMallocManaged(&table, ptrs.size() * sizeof(half*)));
+    for (std::size_t i = 0; i < ptrs.size(); ++i) {
+        table[i] = ptrs[i];
+    }
+    BEAM_CUDA_CHECK(cudaDeviceSynchronize());
+    require_aligned(table, alignof(half*), name);
+}

+__device__ float stream1_load_scalar_device(const half* ptr, std::uint64_t idx, std::uint32_t dtype) {
+    if (dtype == STREAM1_DTYPE_BF16) {
+        return __bfloat162float(reinterpret_cast<const __nv_bfloat16*>(ptr)[idx]);
+    }
+    return __half2float(ptr[idx]);
+}
+
+__device__ void stream1_store_scalar_device(half* ptr, std::uint64_t idx, float value, std::uint32_t dtype) {
+    if (dtype == STREAM1_DTYPE_BF16) {
+        reinterpret_cast<__nv_bfloat16*>(ptr)[idx] = __float2bfloat16(value);
+        return;
+    }
+    ptr[idx] = __float2half(value);
+}

+
+__global__ void stream1_layernorm_relu_kernel(
+    half* matrix,
+    const half* bias,
+    const half* gamma,
+    const half* beta,
+    std::uint32_t rows,
+    std::uint32_t cols,
+    std::uint32_t dtype) {
+    const std::uint32_t row = blockIdx.x;
+    if (row >= rows) {
+        return;
+    }
+    extern __shared__ float reduce[];
+    const std::uint64_t base = static_cast<std::uint64_t>(row) * cols;
+    float sum = 0.0f;
+    for (std::uint32_t col = threadIdx.x; col < cols; col += blockDim.x) {
+        sum += stream1_load_scalar_device(matrix, base + col, dtype) +
+               stream1_load_scalar_device(bias, col, dtype);
+    }
+    reduce[threadIdx.x] = sum;
+    __syncthreads();
+    for (std::uint32_t stride = blockDim.x / 2U; stride > 0U; stride >>= 1U) {
+        if (threadIdx.x < stride) {
+            reduce[threadIdx.x] += reduce[threadIdx.x + stride];
+        }
+        __syncthreads();
+    }
+    const float mean = reduce[0] / static_cast<float>(cols);
+    float var_sum = 0.0f;
+    for (std::uint32_t col = threadIdx.x; col < cols; col += blockDim.x) {
+        const float x = stream1_load_scalar_device(matrix, base + col, dtype) +
+                        stream1_load_scalar_device(bias, col, dtype);
+        const float d = x - mean;
+        var_sum += d * d;
+    }
+    reduce[threadIdx.x] = var_sum;
+    __syncthreads();
+    for (std::uint32_t stride = blockDim.x / 2U; stride > 0U; stride >>= 1U) {
+        if (threadIdx.x < stride) {
+            reduce[threadIdx.x] += reduce[threadIdx.x + stride];
+        }
+        __syncthreads();
+    }
+    const float inv_std = rsqrtf(reduce[0] / static_cast<float>(cols) + 1.0e-5f);

+        reinterpret_cast<__nv_bfloat16*>(ptr)[idx] = __float2bfloat16(value);
+        return;
+    }
+    ptr[idx] = __float2half(value);
+}

+__global__ void stream1_layernorm_relu_kernel(
+    half* matrix,

+    }
+    extern __shared__ float reduce[];
+    const std::uint64_t base = static_cast<std::uint64_t>(row) * cols;
+    float sum = 0.0f;
+    for (std::uint32_t col = threadIdx.x; col < cols; col += blockDim.x) {
+        sum += stream1_load_scalar_device(matrix, base + col, dtype) +
+               stream1_load_scalar_device(bias, col, dtype);
+    }
+    reduce[threadIdx.x] = sum;
+    __syncthreads();
+    for (std::uint32_t stride = blockDim.x / 2U; stride > 0U; stride >>= 1U) {
+        if (threadIdx.x < stride) {

+        }
+        __syncthreads();
+    }
+    const float mean = reduce[0] / static_cast<float>(cols);
+    float var_sum = 0.0f;

FROM nvidia/cuda:12.8.1-cudnn-devel-ubuntu22.04

ENV DEBIAN_FRONTEND=noninteractive
ENV NVIDIA_VISIBLE_DEVICES=all
ENV NVIDIA_DRIVER_CAPABILITIES=compute,utility
ENV PYTHONUNBUFFERED=1

RUN apt-get update \
    && apt-get install -y --no-install-recommends \
        build-essential \
        cmake \
        ninja-build \
        ca-certificates \
        git \
        python3 \
        nsight-systems-2025.6.3 \
        nsight-compute-2026.1.1 \
    && rm -rf /var/lib/apt/lists/*

RUN git clone --depth 1 https://github.com/NVIDIA/cutlass.git /opt/cutlass \
    && test -f /opt/cutlass/include/cutlass/gemm/device/gemm.h

RUN ln -s /usr/bin/python3 /usr/local/bin/python \
    && command -v python \
    && command -v cmake \
    && command -v ninja \
    && command -v nsys \
    && command -v ncu

WORKDIR /work

CMD ["bash", "-lc", "cmake -S . -B build-docker -G Ninja -DCMAKE_BUILD_TYPE=Release && cmake --build build-docker && ctest --test-dir build-docker --output-on-failure"]

gpu-dev-cutlass-nsight:2026-05-24 6.89GB
gpu-dev-cutlass-nsight:cuda128-sm120 20.2GB
gpu-dev:latest 21.2GB

ENV DEBIAN_FRONTEND=noninteractive
ENV NVIDIA_VISIBLE_DEVICES=all
ENV NVIDIA_DRIVER_CAPABILITIES=compute,utility
ENV PYTHONUNBUFFERED=1

RUN apt-get update \
    && apt-get install -y --no-install-recommends \
        build-essential \
        cmake \
        ninja-build \
        ca-certificates \
        git \
        python3 \
        nsight-systems-2025.6.3 \
        nsight-compute-2026.1.1 \

RUN ln -s /usr/bin/python3 /usr/local/bin/python \

*** Update File: memory/CHANGELOG.md
@@
 - Added `tools/compare_solution_lengths.py`, a lightweight solution-length comparison CLI. It compares move counts only, without puzzle validation, from a baseline submission CSV against either another submission CSV or `solve_then_reflect` SLURM logs; output includes old length, new length, delta, source, solution, and an aggregate delta/improved/equal/worsened summary.
+- Extended Stream1 model support for Artgor-style `ResMLPDistance` exports. `tools/export_stream1_mlp.py` now supports `--format resmlp-layernorm` and `--dtype fp16|bf16`, folds the embedding plus first linear layer into the existing position-class input table, exports LayerNorm parameters, and writes manifests with `normalization=layernorm`. Stream1 runtime manifests now accept `dtype=bf16` and optional LayerNorm metadata, and the main CUTLASS Stream1 path inserts LayerNorm kernels between GEMM stages when required while preserving the existing no-LayerNorm fp16 behavior.
*** End Patch

# Project Agent Rules

## Operating Rule
- Read `AGENTS.md` before project edits.
- Update `memory/CHANGELOG.md` after meaningful code, config, or architecture changes.
- Update `memory/PROMPTS.md` with user prompts that define requirements.
- Store test outputs, logs, and verification notes under `test_results/`.
- Prefer agent-centered code: explicit contracts, deterministic tests, small files, low hidden state.

## Architecture Source
- Primary architecture contract: `ARCHITECTURE_NEED.md`.
- Current Stream 4 implementation rule: use threshold + compact + CUB/fixed-temp sort/reduce + compact; do not use Stream 4 hash-table dedup.

## Implementation Guardrails
- `State128` logical bytes are `v[0..119]`.
- `State128` padding bytes are `v[120..127]`.
- Persistent frontier padding must be zero.
- `FinalResponse` may temporarily store `target_local_idx` in `v[120..123]`.
- `Hash128` is one logical 128-bit key stored as `{lo, hi}`.
- `CandidateMeta` must remain 32 bytes and 32-byte aligned.
- Stream 3 payload id must be original candidate id, not compact index.
- Stream 4 must not apply shard top-k or semantic shard cap.

# Project Agent Rules

## Operating Rule
- Read `AGENTS.md` before project edits.
- Update `memory/CHANGELOG.md` after meaningful code, config, or architecture changes.
- Update `memory/PROMPTS.md` with user prompts that define requirements.
- Store test outputs, logs, and verification notes under `test_results/`.
- Prefer agent-centered code: explicit contracts, deterministic tests, small files, low hidden state.

## Architecture Source
- Primary architecture contract: `ARCHITECTURE_NEED.md`.
- Current Stream 4 implementation rule: use threshold + compact + CUB/fixed-temp sort/reduce + compact; do not use Stream 4 hash-table dedup.

## Implementation Guardrails
- `State128` logical bytes are `v[0..119]`.
- `State128` padding bytes are `v[120..127]`.
- Persistent frontier padding must be zero.
- `FinalResponse` may temporarily store `target_local_idx` in `v[120..123]`.
- `Hash128` is one logical 128-bit key stored as `{lo, hi}`.
- `CandidateMeta` must remain 32 bytes and 32-byte aligned.
- Stream 3 payload id must be original candidate id, not compact index.
- Stream 4 must not apply shard top-k or semantic shard cap.

Что добавлено:
- `tools/export_stream1_mlp.py`: `.pt -> stream1_weights/*.fp16|*.bf16`
- формат `--format resmlp-layernorm` для Artgor `ResMLPDistance`
- dtype `--dtype bf16` для A100/H100 и `--dtype fp16` для T4
- runtime читает `normalization=layernorm`, `dtype=bf16`
- поддержан `output_dim=1`
- схема сохранена: `Linear -> LayerNorm -> ReLU -> Linear -> LayerNorm -> ReLU -> residual blocks -> head`
- input bias не дублируется: для LN он добавляется ровно внутри LayerNorm, как в PyTorch

Проверил:
- `python` AST exporter OK
- синтетический Artgor-style checkpoint экспортируется в bf16
- `production_runner` собрался в Docker через `gpu-dev-cutlass-nsight:2026-05-24`
- `git diff --check` чистый

Команда экспорта для A100/H100:

```bash
python tools/export_stream1_mlp.py \
  --weights m_az_v4_v_only.pt \
  --out stream1_weights_artgor_bf16 \
  --format resmlp-layernorm \
  --dtype bf16
```

Для T4:

```bash
python tools/export_stream1_mlp.py \
  --weights m_az_v4_v_only.pt \
  --out stream1_weights_artgor_fp16 \
  --format resmlp-layernorm \
  --dtype fp16
```

Для запуска потом ставить:

```bash
export BEAM_WEIGHT_DIR=/path/to/stream1_weights_artgor_bf16
```

Важно: `gpu-dev:latest` у тебя без `/opt/cutlass`, поэтому для сборочной проверки использовал уже существующий образ с CUTLASS. GPU-runtime локально не гонял, потому что Docker здесь не видит NVIDIA driver.

__global__ void stream1_bias_relu_kernel(
    half* matrix,
    const half* bias,
    std::uint32_t rows,
    std::uint32_t cols) {
    const std::uint32_t i = blockIdx.x * blockDim.x + threadIdx.x;
    const std::uint32_t total = rows * cols;
    if (i >= total) {
        return;
    }
    const std::uint32_t col = i % cols;
    const float x = __half2float(matrix[i]) + __half2float(bias[col]);
    matrix[i] = __float2half(relu_device(x));
}

The validator reproduced the depth-7 failure before materialization with `invalid_count=278`; the first bad final request had `parent_idx=6438027017329790029` while `current_frontier_size=100007936`, with valid `target_local_idx`, move, and request slot. The scan fails before `final_filter_load_balance_cuda`, proving the corrupt `CandidateMeta` is already in live `survivor_shard`: `invalid_selected_count=104`, `first_shard=3`, `first_local=233861`, `parent_idx=6293324596111102288`, `current_frontier_size=21612910`, `score_key=0`, `route_packed=0`. The bad bytes look like stale `State128`/next-frontier materialization data being counted as live survivor candidates after phase3 scratch overlay, so the next root-cause target is Stream3/Stream4 count/write agreement for survivor slots. Verification: Docker debug `production_runner` build passed. Report updated at `test_results/molab_p992_final_validate_2026-06-03.md`.
- Fixed the root cause of the Molab <truncated omitted_approx_tokens="3301" />MEPhI tuning launcher issues found on `basis`: SLURM spools submitted scripts under `/var/lib/slurm/slurmd/job...`, so the tuning scripts now source `mephi_8xa100_common.sh` from `${SLURM_SUBMIT_DIR:-$(pwd)}` instead of `BASH_SOURCE[0]`; `tune_8xa100_stream1.sh` now parses `b_micro=` from `stream_benchmark` output when writing `logs/best_stream1.env`.
- Fixed the MEPhI pipeline sweep average-depth parser (`printf "%.6f", sum / n`) and added automatic per-config GPU utilization logging. `beam_torchrun_production` now runs a background `nvidia-smi` sampler every 5 seconds while torchrun is active and writes `logs/tuning_<job>/nvidia_smi_<run_tag>.log`, then stops the sampler after the config exits.
- Hardened MEPhI pipeline history cleanup: the sweep now clears history through `beam_safe_clear_history_contents`, which requires `realpath(HISTORY_DIR) == realpath(JOB_DIR)/history` and removes only first-level entries inside that directory. This replaces the direct `rm -rf "${HISTORY_DIR:?}/"*` sweep cleanup.
- Added `tools/compare_solution_lengths.py`, a lightweight solution-length comparison CLI. It compares move counts only, without puzzle validation, from a baseline submission CSV against either another submission CSV or `solve_then_reflect` SLURM logs; output includes old length, new length, delta, source, solution, and an aggregate delta/improved/equal/worsened summary.
- Extended Stream1 model support for Artgor-style `ResMLPDistance` exports. `tools/export_stream1_mlp.py` now supports `--format resmlp-layernorm` and `--dtype fp16|bf16`, folds the embedding plus first linear layer into the existing position-class input table, exports LayerNorm parameters, and writes manifests with `normalization=layernorm`. Stream1 runtime manifests now accept `dtype=bf16` and optional LayerNorm metadata, and the main CUTLASS Stream1 path inserts LayerNorm kernels between GEMM stages when required while preserving the existing no-LayerNorm fp16 behavior.

- формат `--format resmlp-layernorm` для Artgor `ResMLPDistance`
- dtype `--dtype bf16` для A100/H100 и `--dtype fp16` для T4
- runtime читает `normalization=layernorm`, `dtype=bf16`
- поддержан `output_dim=1`
- схема сохранена: `Linear -> LayerNorm -> ReLU -> Linear -> LayerNorm -> ReLU -> residual blocks -> head`
- input bias не дублируется: для LN он добавляется ровно внутри LayerNorm, как в PyTorch

Проверил:
- `python` AST exporter OK
- синтетический Artgor-style checkpoint экспортируется в bf16
- `production_runner` собрался в Docker через `gpu-dev-cutlass-nsight:2026-05-24`
- `git diff --check` чистый

Команда экспорта для A100/H100:

```bash
python tools/export_stream1_mlp.py \
  --weights m_az_v4_v_only.pt \
  --out stream1_weights_artgor_bf16 \
  --format resmlp-layernorm \
  --dtype bf16
```

Для T4:

```bash
python tools/export_stream1_mlp.py \
  --weights m_az_v4_v_only.pt \
  --out stream1_weights_artgor_fp16 \
  --format resmlp-layernorm \
  --dtype fp16
```

Для запуска потом ставить:

```bash
export BEAM_WEIGHT_DIR=/path/to/stream1_weights_artgor_bf16
```

Важно: `gpu-dev:latest` у тебя без `/opt/cutlass`, поэтому для сборочной проверки использовал уже существующий образ с CUTLASS. GPU-runtime локально не гонял, потому что Docker здесь не видит NVIDIA driver.

    const float x = __half2float(matrix[i]) + __half2float(bias[col]);
    matrix[i] = __float2half(relu_device(x));
}

-- Extended Stream1 model support for Artgor-style `ResMLPDistance` exports. `tools/export_stream1_mlp.py` now supports `--format resmlp-layernorm` and `--dtype fp16|bf16`, folds the embedding plus first linear layer into the existing position-class input table, exports LayerNorm parameters, and writes manifests with `normalization=layernorm`. Stream1 runtime manifests now accept `dtype=bf16` and optional LayerNorm metadata, and the main CUTLASS Stream1 path inserts LayerNorm kernels between GEMM stages when required while preserving the existing no-LayerNorm fp16 behavior. The LayerNorm input path keeps folded input pre-bias and applies `input_bias` only inside LayerNorm to match PyTorch `Linear -> LayerNorm -> ReLU` semantics.
+- Extended Stream1 model support for Artgor-style `ResMLPDistance` exports. `tools/export_stream1_mlp.py` now supports `--format resmlp-layernorm` and `--dtype fp16|bf16`, folds the embedding plus first linear layer into the existing position-class input table, exports LayerNorm parameters, and writes manifests with `normalization=layernorm`. Stream1 runtime manifests now accept `dtype=bf16` and optional LayerNorm metadata, and the main CUTLASS Stream1 path inserts LayerNorm kernels between GEMM stages when required while preserving the existing no-LayerNorm fp16 behavior. The LayerNorm input path keeps folded input pre-bias and applies `input_bias` only inside LayerNorm to match PyTorch `Linear -> LayerNorm -> ReLU` semantics; the existing fp16 no-LayerNorm folded-input path keeps `half2` bias/weight reads.

[431] assistant: Да, `half2` был нужен для скорости в folded input kernel: читать/конвертить пары `fp16` весов и bias за раз.

Я поправил: старый быстрый путь `fp16 + no LayerNorm` снова использует `half2` для bias/weight reads. Новый scalar dtype-aware путь остается только для случаев, где он нужен: `bf16` и LayerNorm-семантика.

Итог по скорости:
- старая `fp16` модель без LayerNorm не должна просесть из-за этой правки;
- Artgor `bf16 + LayerNorm` будет иметь дополнительную цену LayerNorm kernels, но это уже цена самой архитектуры модели, не fallback и не случайная деградация старого пути.

Проверка: `production_runner` пересобрался в Docker успешно, `git diff --check` чистый.

---
name: work-with-kaggle
description: Use when the user asks Codex to work with Kaggle, kaggle.com, Kaggle notebooks/kernels/datasets, Kaggle CLI, 2xT4/H100 notebook validation, kernel push/status/output/downloads, or Kaggle run diagnostics. This includes requests like "Р·Р°РїСѓСЃС‚Рё РЅР° РєР°РіР»Рµ", "РїСЂРѕРІРµСЂСЊ Kaggle logs", "push kernel", "download Kaggle output", "СЃРґРµР»Р°Р№ T4x2 notebook", "РѕРїСѓР±Р»РёРєСѓР№ notebook", or "watch Kaggle status".
---

# Work With Kaggle

Use this skill whenever the task involves Kaggle notebooks, kernels, datasets, or GPU validation runs.

## Project Memory First

For project repositories, read local project guidance before changing notebooks or source:

- `AGENTS.md`
- `memory/PROMPTS.md` and `memory/CHANGELOG.md`, especially recent Kaggle entries
- relevant `test_results/kaggle_*` reports for the same failure/config family

This project has many Kaggle-specific decisions preserved in memory. Do not assume a generic Kaggle workflow is correct when project memory says otherwise.

## Safety And Scope

- Do not upload or publish current uncommitted source to a Kaggle dataset unless the user explicitly approves external export.
- Prefer GitHub-backed Kaggle source when the user has selected GitHub as source of truth.
- Do not push a public Kaggle notebook/kernel unless the user explicitly approves publication or public push.
- Do not make C++/CUDA algorithmic changes while diagnosing Kaggle failures unless the user explicitly approves code changes. Config-only notebook changes are acceptable when useful and consistent with the request.
- Do not revert unrelated local changes. Kaggle notebook edits often coexist with dirty source changes.
- For multi-rank validation, do not fake `WORLD_SIZE=2` on one GPU with both ranks on `LOCAL_RANK=0` unless the user explici<truncated omitted_approx_tokens="692" />ed logs. This rule came from project memory and applies to diagnostic/watch tasks.
- Do not stop production/no-timeout runs unless the user explicitly asks or the notebook itself has a configured timeout.
- If a run appears hung because stdout is redirected, check rank log files and whether live-rank streaming is enabled before assuming a stall.
- Preserve logs even when stopping: terminate gracefully when possible, then download output files.

## Failure Diagnosis Checklist

After a Kaggle failure, collect and scan:

- Kaggle worker status and kernel version
- notebook stdout tail
- rank logs under the notebook output directory
- result JSONL/CSV summaries
- GPU budget/preflight output
- explicit Kaggle messages such as disk quota failures

Common patterns in this project:

- `KernelWorkerStatus.ERROR` plus "more disk space than is available" usually means disk quota/static history arena pressure.
- `return_code=-9` can indicate host RAM OOM.
- `code=3002` / `STREAM_FATAL_STREAM3_DOUBLE_BUFFER_OVERFLOW` points to Stream3/Stream4/threshold/capacity flow diagnostics.
- `final selected count does not match score phase counts` points to final selection/histogram/exact-count mismatch.
- `cudaGraphInstantiate: out of memory` may require config-only headroom/shard-capacity changes before deeper code work.
- Silent output after build may simply mean rank stdout is redirected and live rank logging is disabled.

Write a short report under `test_results/` for meaningful Kaggle validations, sweeps, failures, or launches.

## Response Style

- Be explicit about whether you changed source code, notebook config only, or only launched/monitored a run.
- Include Kaggle kernel slug/version/status when known.
- Include exact local report/log paths in `test_results/`.
- Keep recommendations tied to evidence from logs, not guesses.
- If the next step requires exporting source or publishing publicly, ask for explicit approval first.

name: mephi-hpc-work-rules

---

# MEPhI HPC Work Rules

Use this skill before giving or running commands for MEPhI HPC work.

## Operating Identity

- Entity: `mephi_hpc_work_rules`
- Type: `operational_rules`
- State: `active`
- Account login: `vokirova`
- Entry command: `ssh mephi`
- Current priority second hop: `ssh basis`
- Current priority partition: `kaf12`
- Current submit command: `sbatch -p kaf12 start.sh`
- Job data root: `/mnt/pool/6/vokirova`
- User software roots: `/mnt/pool/3/vokirova`, `/mnt/pool/4/vokirova`
- Visible terminal gateway: one user-visible `cmd.exe`, Windows Terminal, or
  equivalent terminal session where the user can see every MEPhI HPC command and
  every output line.

## Non-Negotiable Safety Rules

Never reveal, print, copy into chat, upload, or send:

- the private key file contents;
- the private key passphrase;
- any command output that contains private key material or passphrase text.

The private key path may be referenced as configuration context:

```text
~/.ssh/MEPHI/id_rsa_mephi_hpc
```

The public key `.pub` may be sent only to MEPhI HPC administrators for access
registration or key replacement. Do not dump it into chat unless the user is
explicitly preparing an administrator-facing message and the destination is
clear.

Do not ask the user to type a passphrase into chat. If SSH prompts for a
passphrase, the user handles that prompt locally in the terminal.

## Visible Terminal Gateway Rule

Before doing MEPhI HPC work, create or identify one visible terminal gateway.
This is a terminal window on the user's screen, usually `cmd.exe` or Windows
Terminal, where the session is connected with:

```bash
ssh mephi
ssh basis
``<truncated omitted_approx_tokens="1812" />mephi:/mnt/pool/6/vokirova/<jobname>/slurm-<id>.out <local-path>
```

If syncing large trees to `/mnt/pool/6`, avoid expensive delta scans when
possible. Prefer explicit scoped transfers, and when `rsync` is necessary use
options such as:

```bash
rsync --whole-file --inplace
```

## Response Behavior For Codex

Before proposing or running HPC commands:

1. Check whether a single visible terminal gateway is active and route cluster
   work through it.
2. If the gateway is missing, open/create it first; if its launcher is outdated,
   update the launcher first.
3. If Codex will paste/type commands, verify the target prompt or have the user
   paste the commands manually.
4. Classify the action as setup, transfer, compile, queue inspection, or
   computation.
5. If it is computation, convert it into a SLURM job script and `sbatch`
   submission.
6. Keep every job under `/mnt/pool/6/vokirova/<jobname>`.
7. Select a partition according to the policy above.
8. Avoid stdout/stderr redirection for beginners unless there is a clear reason.
9. Always tell the user the job id and output file path after submission.

If a requested action would violate these rules, refuse that action briefly and
provide the compliant SLURM-based alternative.

## Source Snapshot

Primary sources:

- MEPhI HPC FAQ: `https://it.mephi.ru/hpc/faq`
- OpenSSH Unix: `https://it.mephi.ru/hpc/openssh`
- OpenSSH WSL: `https://it.mephi.ru/hpc/openssh-wsl`
- PuTTY note: `https://it.mephi.ru/hpc/putty`
- Account request/renewal: `https://it.mephi.ru/hpc/join`
- HPC usage rules: `https://it.mephi.ru/hpc/usage`
- HPC resources: `https://it.mephi.ru/hpc/perfomance`
- User guide PDF: `https://ut.mephi.ru/pdf/projects/hpc/unicluster/userguide.pdf`
- Mailing list: `https://lists.mephi.ru/listinfo/hpc`

Local reference snapshot:

- `mephi_hpc_work_rules_sources.md`
- `docs/deployment/mephi_hpc_operating_rules.md`
- `docs/contracts/SUMMARY.md`

beam_export_common_runtime() {
  export OMP_NUM_THREADS="${SLURM_CPUS_PER_TASK:-8}"
  export NCCL_DEBUG="${NCCL_DEBUG:-WARN}"
  export NCCL_ASYNC_ERROR_HANDLING=1
  export CUDA_DEVICE_MAX_CONNECTIONS=32
  export BEAM_WEIGHT_DIR="${REPO_DIR}/stream1_weights"
  export BEAM_DEPTH_LOG_EVERY=1
  export BEAM_HISTORY_MODE="${BEAM_HISTORY_MODE:-static_hybrid}"
  export BEAM_HISTORY_SLOT_COUNT="${BEAM_HISTORY_SLOT_COUNT:-2}"
  export BEAM_HISTORY_WORKERS="${BEAM_HISTORY_WORKERS:-4}"
  export BEAM_HISTORY_RAM_BYTES="${BEAM_HISTORY_RAM_BYTES:-$((160 * 1024 * 1024 * 1024))}"
  export BEAM_HISTORY_DISK_BYTES="${BEAM_HISTORY_DISK_BYTES:-$((384 * 1024 * 1024 * 1024))}"
  export BEAM_HISTORY_DISK_PATH="${HISTORY_DIR}"
  export BEAM_SOLVED_NEIGHBORHOOD_RADIUS="${BEAM_SOLVED_NEIGHBORHOOD_RADIUS:-5}"
  export BEAM_SOLVED_NEIGHBORHOOD_MAX_ENTRIES="${BEAM_SOLVED_NEIGHBORHOOD_MAX_ENTRIES:-3000000}"
  export BEAM_STREAM2_SUFFIX_RADIUS="${BEAM_STREAM2_SUFFIX_RADIUS:-0}"
  export BEAM_STREAM2_SUFFIX_BACKEND="${BEAM_STREAM2_SUFFIX_BACKEND:-composed_permutations}"
  export BEAM_STREAM2_SUFFIX_MAX_COUNT="${BEAM_STREAM2_SUFFIX_MAX_COUNT:-1}"
  export BEAM_PREDICT_STATS_VERBOSE="${BEAM_PREDICT_STATS_VERBOSE:-0}"
}

beam_export_manual_config() {
  export BEAM_RUNTIME_CONFIG_MODE=manual
  export BEAM_SHARD_COUNT="${SHARD_COUNT}"
  export BEAM_STREAM4_BATCH_CANDIDATE<truncated omitted_approx_tokens="147" />CHANGE_SCALE_PPM="${BEAM_FINAL_MATERIALIZE_EXCHANGE_SCALE_PPM:-1200000}"
  export BEAM_GPU_HEADROOM_BYTES="${BEAM_GPU_HEADROOM_BYTES:-$((3 * 1024 * 1024 * 1024))}"
}

beam_prepare_nccl_file() {
  NCCL_ID_FILE="${JOB_DIR}/beam_solver_nccl_${SLURM_JOB_ID:-manual}_${1:-run}.bin"
  rm -f "${NCCL_ID_FILE}"
  export BEAM_NCCL_ID_FILE="${NCCL_ID_FILE}"
}

beam_torchrun_production() {
  local run_tag="$1"
  local run_log="$2"
  local rank_log_dir="${LOG_DIR}/ranks_${SLURM_JOB_ID:-manual}_${run_tag}"
  local gpu_log="${TUNING_DIR}/nvidia_smi_${run_tag}.log"
  local gpu_monitor_pid=""
  mkdir -p "${rank_log_dir}"
  export BEAM_RANK_LOG_DIR="${rank_log_dir}"
  echo "run_tag=${run_tag}"
  echo "run_log=${run_log}"
  echo "rank_log_dir=${BEAM_RANK_LOG_DIR}"
  echo "beam_nccl_id_file=${BEAM_NCCL_ID_FILE}"
  echo "gpu_monitor_log=${gpu_log}"
  (
    while true; do
      date -Is
      nvidia-smi --query-gpu=index,name,temperature.gpu,power.draw,memory.used,memory.total,utilization.gpu,utilization.memory --format=csv,noheader,nounits
      sleep 5
    done
  ) > "${gpu_log}" 2>&1 &
  gpu_monitor_pid=$!
  set +e
  "${NINJA_VENV_DIR}/bin/python" -m torch.distributed.run \
    --nnodes="${TORCHRUN_NNODES}" \
    --nproc-per-node="${TORCHRUN_NPROC_PER_NODE}" \
    --node-rank="${TORCHRUN_NODE_RANK}" \
    --rdzv-backend=c10d \
    --rdzv-endpoint="${TORCHRUN_RDZV_ENDPOINT}" \
    --rdzv-id="beam8a100_${SLURM_JOB_ID:-manual}_${run_tag}" \
    --no-python \
    /bin/bash -lc 'if [ "${RANK:-0}" = "0" ]; then exec "$@"; else exec "$@" > "${BEAM_RANK_LOG_DIR}/rank${RANK}.log" 2>&1; fi' \
    bash "${BUILD_DIR}/production_runner" "${PUZZLE_ID}" "${DEPTH_LIMIT}" "${BEAM_WIDTH}" 2>&1 | tee "${run_log}"
  local torchrun_rc=${PIPESTATUS[0]}
  set -e
  if [ -n "${gpu_monitor_pid}" ]; then
    kill "${gpu_monitor_pid}" >/dev/null 2>&1 || true
    wait "${gpu_monitor_pid}" >/dev/null 2>&1 || true
  fi
  return "${torchrun_rc}"
}

[431] assistant: Да, `half2` был нужен для скорости в folded input kernel: читать/конвертить пары `fp16` весов и bias за раз.

Я поправил: старый быстрый путь `fp16 + no LayerNorm` снова использует `half2` для bias/weight reads. Новый scalar dtype-aware путь остается только для случаев, где он нужен: `bf16` и LayerNorm-семантика.

Итог по скорости:
- старая `fp16` модель без LayerNorm не должна просесть из-за этой правки;
- Artgor `bf16 + LayerNorm` будет иметь дополнительную цену LayerNorm kernels, но это уже цена самой архитектуры модели, не fallback и не случайная деградация старого пути.

Проверка: `production_runner` пересобрался в Docker успешно, `git diff --check` чистый.

name: work-with-kaggle
description: Use when the user asks Codex to work with Kaggle, kaggle.com, Kaggle notebooks/kernels/datasets, Kaggle CLI, 2xT4/H100 notebook validation, kernel push/status/output/downloads, or Kaggle run diagnostics. This includes requests like "Р·Р°РїСѓСЃС‚Рё РЅР° РєР°РіР»Рµ", "РїСЂРѕРІРµСЂСЊ Kaggle logs", "push kernel", "download Kaggle output", "СЃРґРµР»Р°Р№ T4x2 notebook", "РѕРїСѓР±Р»РёРєСѓР№ notebook", or "watch Kaggle status".

# Work With Kaggle

Use this skill whenever the task involves Kaggle notebooks, kernels, datasets, or GPU validation runs.

## Project Memory First

For project repositories, read local project guidance before changing notebooks or source:

- `AGENTS.md`
- `memory/PROMPTS.md` and `memory/CHANGELOG.md`, especially recent Kaggle entries
- relevant `test_results/kaggle_*` reports for the same failure/config family

This project has many Kaggle-specific decisions preserved in memory. Do not assume a generic Kaggle workflow is correct when project memory says otherwise.

## Safety And Scope

- Do not upload or publish current uncommitted source to a Kaggle dataset unless the user explicitly approves external export.
- Prefer GitHub-backed Kaggle source when the user has selected GitHub as source of truth.
- Do not push a public Kaggle notebook/kernel unless the user explicitly approves publication or public push.
- Do not make C++/CUDA algorithmic changes while diagnosing Kaggle failures unless the user explicitly approves code changes. Config-only notebook changes are acceptable when useful and consistent with the request.
- Do not revert unrelated local changes. Kaggle notebook edits often coexist with dirty source changes.
- For multi-rank validation, do not fake `WORLD_SIZE=2` on one GPU with both ranks on `LOCAL_RANK=0` unless the user explici<truncated omitted_approx_tokens="692" />ed logs. This rule came from project memory and applies to diagnostic/watch tasks.
- Do not stop production/no-timeout runs unless the user explicitly asks or the notebook itself has a configured timeout.
- If a run appears hung because stdout is redirected, check rank log files and whether live-rank streaming is enabled before assuming a stall.
- Preserve logs even when stopping: terminate gracefully when possible, then download output files.

## Failure Diagnosis Checklist

After a Kaggle failure, collect and scan:

- Kaggle worker status and kernel version
- notebook stdout tail
- rank logs under the notebook output directory
- result JSONL/CSV summaries
- GPU budget/preflight output
- explicit Kaggle messages such as disk quota failures

Common patterns in this project:

- `KernelWorkerStatus.ERROR` plus "more disk space than is available" usually means disk quota/static history arena pressure.
- `return_code=-9` can indicate host RAM OOM.
- `code=3002` / `STREAM_FATAL_STREAM3_DOUBLE_BUFFER_OVERFLOW` points to Stream3/Stream4/threshold/capacity flow diagnostics.
- `final selected count does not match score phase counts` points to final selection/histogram/exact-count mismatch.
- `cudaGraphInstantiate: out of memory` may require config-only headroom/shard-capacity changes before deeper code work.
- Silent output after build may simply mean rank stdout is redirected and live rank logging is disabled.

Write a short report under `test_results/` for meaningful Kaggle validations, sweeps, failures, or launches.

## Response Style

- Be explicit about whether you changed source code, notebook config only, or only launched/monitored a run.
- Include Kaggle kernel slug/version/status when known.
- Include exact local report/log paths in `test_results/`.
- Keep recommendations tied to evidence from logs, not guesses.
- If the next step requires exporting source or publishing publicly, ask for explicit approval first.

---
name: mephi-hpc-work-rules
description: Use when helping with NRNU MEPhI HPC, ssh mephi, vokirova, basis, kaf12, SLURM sbatch jobs, MEPhI cluster file transfers, software setup, or partition/resource selection.
---

# MEPhI HPC Work Rules

Use this skill before giving or running commands for MEPhI HPC work.

## Operating Identity

- Entity: `mephi_hpc_work_rules`
- Type: `operational_rules`
- State: `active`
- Account login: `vokirova`
- Entry command: `ssh mephi`
- Current priority second hop: `ssh basis`
- Current priority partition: `kaf12`
- Current submit command: `sbatch -p kaf12 start.sh`
- Job data root: `/mnt/pool/6/vokirova`
- User software roots: `/mnt/pool/3/vokirova`, `/mnt/pool/4/vokirova`
- Visible terminal gateway: one user-visible `cmd.exe`, Windows Terminal, or
  equivalent terminal session where the user can see every MEPhI HPC command and
  every output line.

## Non-Negotiable Safety Rules

Never reveal, print, copy into chat, upload, or send:

- the private key file contents;
- the private key passphrase;
- any command output that contains private key material or passphrase text.

The private key path may be referenced as configuration context:

```text
~/.ssh/MEPHI/id_rsa_mephi_hpc
```

The public key `.pub` may be sent only to MEPhI HPC administrators for access
registration or key replacement. Do not dump it into chat unless the user is
explicitly preparing an administrator-facing message and the destination is
clear.

Do not ask the user to type a passphrase into chat. If SSH prompts for a
passphrase, the user handles that prompt locally in the terminal.

## Visible Terminal Gateway Rule

Before doing MEPhI HPC work, create or identify one visible terminal gateway.
This is a terminal window on the user's screen, usually `cmd.exe` or Windows
Terminal, where the session is connected with:

```bash
ssh mephi
ssh basis
``<truncated omitted_approx_tokens="1812" />mephi:/mnt/pool/6/vokirova/<jobname>/slurm-<id>.out <local-path>
```

If syncing large trees to `/mnt/pool/6`, avoid expensive delta scans when
possible. Prefer explicit scoped transfers, and when `rsync` is necessary use
options such as:

```bash
rsync --whole-file --inplace
```

## Response Behavior For Codex

Before proposing or running HPC commands:

1. Check whether a single visible terminal gateway is active and route cluster
   work through it.
2. If the gateway is missing, open/create it first; if its launcher is outdated,
   update the launcher first.
3. If Codex will paste/type commands, verify the target prompt or have the user
   paste the commands manually.
4. Classify the action as setup, transfer, compile, queue inspection, or
   computation.
5. If it is computation, convert it into a SLURM job script and `sbatch`
   submission.
6. Keep every job under `/mnt/pool/6/vokirova/<jobname>`.
7. Select a partition according to the policy above.
8. Avoid stdout/stderr redirection for beginners unless there is a clear reason.
9. Always tell the user the job id and output file path after submission.

If a requested action would violate these rules, refuse that action briefly and
provide the compliant SLURM-based alternative.

## Source Snapshot

Primary sources:

- MEPhI HPC FAQ: `https://it.mephi.ru/hpc/faq`
- OpenSSH Unix: `https://it.mephi.ru/hpc/openssh`
- OpenSSH WSL: `https://it.mephi.ru/hpc/openssh-wsl`
- PuTTY note: `https://it.mephi.ru/hpc/putty`
- Account request/renewal: `https://it.mephi.ru/hpc/join`
- HPC usage rules: `https://it.mephi.ru/hpc/usage`
- HPC resources: `https://it.mephi.ru/hpc/perfomance`
- User guide PDF: `https://ut.mephi.ru/pdf/projects/hpc/unicluster/userguide.pdf`
- Mailing list: `https://lists.mephi.ru/listinfo/hpc`

Local reference snapshot:

- `mephi_hpc_work_rules_sources.md`
- `docs/deployment/mephi_hpc_operating_rules.md`
- `docs/contracts/SUMMARY.md`

    echo "invalid_stream3_batch=${STREAM3_BATCH_CANDIDATES} shard_capacity=${SHARD_CAPACITY_CANDIDATES}"
    return 2
  fi
  if [ "${BEAM_STREAM1_CONCURRENCY}" -gt "${BEAM_STREAM3_RING_SLOTS}" ]; then
    echo "invalid_stream1_concurrency=${BEAM_STREAM1_CONCURRENCY} stream3_ring_slots=${BEAM_STREAM3_RING_SLOTS}"
    return 2
  fi
}

beam_export_common_runtime() {
  export OMP_NUM_THREADS="${SLURM_CPUS_PER_TASK:-8}"
  export NCCL_DEBUG="${NCCL_DEBUG:-WARN}"
  export NCCL_ASYNC_ERROR_HANDLING=1
  export CUDA_DEVICE_MAX_CONNECTIONS=32
  export BEAM_WEIGHT_DIR="${REPO_DIR}/stream1_weights"
  export BEAM_DEPTH_LOG_EVERY=1
  export BEAM_HISTORY_MODE="${BEAM_HISTORY_MODE:-static_hybrid}"
  export BEAM_HISTORY_SLOT_COUNT="${BEAM_HISTORY_SLOT_COUNT:-2}"
  export BEAM_HISTORY_WORKERS="${BEAM_HISTORY_WORKERS:-4}"
  export BEAM_HISTORY_RAM_BYTES="${BEAM_HISTORY_RAM_BYTES:-$((160 * 1024 * 1024 * 1024))}"
  export BEAM_HISTORY_DISK_BYTES="${BEAM_HISTORY_DISK_BYTES:-$((384 * 1024 * 1024 * 1024))}"
  export BEAM_HISTORY_DISK_PATH="${HISTORY_DIR}"
  export BEAM_SOLVED_NEIGHBORHOOD_RADIUS="${BEAM_SOLVED_NEIGHBORHOOD_RADIUS:-5}"
  export BEAM_SOLVED_NEIGHBORHOOD_MAX_ENTRIES="${BEAM_SOLVED_NEIGHBORHOOD_MAX_ENTRIES:-3000000}"
  export BEAM_STREAM2_SUFFIX_RADIUS="${BEAM_STREAM2_SUFFIX_RADIUS:-0}"
  export BEAM_STREAM2_SUFFIX_BACKEND="${BEAM_STREAM2_SUFFIX_BACKEND:-composed_permutations}"
  export BEAM_STREAM2_SUFFIX_MAX_COUNT="${BEAM_STREAM2_SUFFIX_MAX_COUNT:-1}"
  export BEAM_PREDICT_STATS_VERBOSE="${BEAM_PREDICT_STATS_VERBOSE:-0}"
}

beam_export_manual_config() {
  export BEAM_RUNTIME_CONFIG_MODE=manual
  export BEAM_SHARD_COUNT="${SHARD_COUNT}"
  export BEAM_STREAM4_BATCH_CANDIDATE<truncated omitted_approx_tokens="147" />CHANGE_SCALE_PPM="${BEAM_FINAL_MATERIALIZE_EXCHANGE_SCALE_PPM:-1200000}"
  export BEAM_GPU_HEADROOM_BYTES="${BEAM_GPU_HEADROOM_BYTES:-$((3 * 1024 * 1024 * 1024))}"
}

beam_prepare_nccl_file() {
  NCCL_ID_FILE="${JOB_DIR}/beam_solver_nccl_${SLURM_JOB_ID:-manual}_${1:-run}.bin"
  rm -f "${NCCL_ID_FILE}"
  export BEAM_NCCL_ID_FILE="${NCCL_ID_FILE}"
}

beam_torchrun_production() {
  local run_tag="$1"
  local run_log="$2"
  local rank_log_dir="${LOG_DIR}/ranks_${SLURM_JOB_ID:-manual}_${run_tag}"
  local gpu_log="${TUNING_DIR}/nvidia_smi_${run_tag}.log"
  local gpu_monitor_pid=""
  mkdir -p "${rank_log_dir}"
  export BEAM_RANK_LOG_DIR="${rank_log_dir}"
  echo "run_tag=${run_tag}"
  echo "run_log=${run_log}"
  echo "rank_log_dir=${BEAM_RANK_LOG_DIR}"
  echo "beam_nccl_id_file=${BEAM_NCCL_ID_FILE}"
  echo "gpu_monitor_log=${gpu_log}"
  (
    while true; do
      date -Is
      nvidia-smi --query-gpu=index,name,temperature.gpu,power.draw,memory.used,memory.total,utilization.gpu,utilization.memory --format=csv,noheader,nounits
      sleep 5
    done
  ) > "${gpu_log}" 2>&1 &
  gpu_monitor_pid=$!
  set +e
  "${NINJA_VENV_DIR}/bin/python" -m torch.distributed.run \
    --nnodes="${TORCHRUN_NNODES}" \
    --nproc-per-node="${TORCHRUN_NPROC_PER_NODE}" \
    --node-rank="${TORCHRUN_NODE_RANK}" \
    --rdzv-backend=c10d \
    --rdzv-endpoint="${TORCHRUN_RDZV_ENDPOINT}" \
    --rdzv-id="beam8a100_${SLURM_JOB_ID:-manual}_${run_tag}" \
    --no-python \
    /bin/bash -lc 'if [ "${RANK:-0}" = "0" ]; then exec "$@"; else exec "$@" > "${BEAM_RANK_LOG_DIR}/rank${RANK}.log" 2>&1; fi' \
    bash "${BUILD_DIR}/production_runner" "${PUZZLE_ID}" "${DEPTH_LIMIT}" "${BEAM_WIDTH}" 2>&1 | tee "${run_log}"
  local torchrun_rc=${PIPESTATUS[0]}
  set -e
  if [ -n "${gpu_monitor_pid}" ]; then
    kill "${gpu_monitor_pid}" >/dev/null 2>&1 || true
    wait "${gpu_monitor_pid}" >/dev/null 2>&1 || true
  fi
  return "${torchrun_rc}"
}

[498] tool apply_patch call: *** Begin Patch
*** Update File: memory/CHANGELOG.md
@@
 - Extended Stream1 model support for Artgor-style `ResMLPDistance` exports. `tools/export_stream1_mlp.py` now supports `--format resmlp-layernorm` and `--dtype fp16|bf16`, folds the embedding plus first linear layer into the existing position-class input table, exports LayerNorm parameters, and writes manifests with `normalization=layernorm`. Stream1 runtime manifests now accept `dtype=bf16` and optional LayerNorm metadata, and the main CUTLASS Stream1 path inserts LayerNorm kernels between GEMM stages when required while preserving the existing no-LayerNorm fp16 behavior. The LayerNorm input path keeps folded input pre-bias and applies `input_bias` only inside LayerNorm to match PyTorch `Linear -> LayerNorm -> ReLU` semantics; the existing fp16 no-LayerNorm folded-input path keeps `half2` bias/weight reads.
+- Downloaded Kaggle checkpoint `artgor/megaminx-tpu-artifacts:m_az_v4_v_only.pt`, exported it as repository weights under `stream1_weights_artgor_bf16/` using `--format resmlp-layernorm --dtype bf16`, and prepared it for MEPhI 8xA100 puzzle `991` runs. `hpc/mephi_8xa100_common.sh` now respects a caller-provided `BEAM_WEIGHT_DIR`, so cluster jobs can select the Artgor weights without replacing the default `stream1_weights` directory.
*** End Patch

[498] tool apply_patch call: *** Begin Patch
*** Update File: memory/CHANGELOG.md
@@
 - Extended Stream1 model support for Artgor-style `ResMLPDistance` exports. `tools/export_stream1_mlp.py` now supports `--format resmlp-layernorm` and `--dtype fp16|bf16`, folds the embedding plus first linear layer into the existing position-class input table, exports LayerNorm parameters, and writes manifests with `normalization=layernorm`. Stream1 runtime manifests now accept `dtype=bf16` and optional LayerNorm metadata, and the main CUTLASS Stream1 path inserts LayerNorm kernels between GEMM stages when required while preserving the existing no-LayerNorm fp16 behavior. The LayerNorm input path keeps folded input pre-bias and applies `input_bias` only inside LayerNorm to match PyTorch `Linear -> LayerNorm -> ReLU` semantics; the existing fp16 no-LayerNorm folded-input path keeps `half2` bias/weight reads.
+- Downloaded Kaggle checkpoint `artgor/megaminx-tpu-artifacts:m_az_v4_v_only.pt`, exported it as repository weights under `stream1_weights_artgor_bf16/` using `--format resmlp-layernorm --dtype bf16`, and prepared it for MEPhI 8xA100 puzzle `991` runs. `hpc/mephi_8xa100_common.sh` now respects a caller-provided `BEAM_WEIGHT_DIR`, so cluster jobs can select the Artgor weights without replacing the default `stream1_weights` directory.
*** End Patch

[588] tool shell_command call: {"command":"@'\nimport json, pathlib\n\ndef load(p):\n    m=json.loads(pathlib.Path(p,'manifest.json').read_text())\n    return dict(h1=m.get('hidden1',m.get('hd1')), h2=m.get('hidden2',m.get('hd2')), nrd=m.get('residual_count',m.get('nrd')), out=m['output_dim'], state=m['state_len'], classes=m['num_classes'])\n\ndef calc(m,b=8192,conc=8):\n    rows_per_parent=24 if m['out']==1 else 1\n    rows=b*rows_per_parent\n    elems_per_row=m['h1']+2*m['h2']+m['out']\n    scratch_lane=rows*elems_per_row*2\n    scratch_total=scratch_lane*conc\n    weights=(m['state']*m['classes']*m['h1'] + m['h1'] + m['h1']*m['h2'] + m['h2'] + 2*m['nrd']*m['h2']*m['h2'] + 2*m['nrd']*m['h2'] + m['h2']*m['out'] + m['out'])*2\n    return rows_per_parent, rows, elems_per_row, scratch_lane, scratch_total, weights\n\ndef gib(x): return x/1024**3\nfor name,path in [('old24','stream1_weights'),('artgor1','stream1_weights_artgor_bf16')]:\n    m=load(path); r=calc(m)\n    print(name,m)\n    print(' rows_per_parent',r[0],'rows_per_lane',r[1],'elems_per_row',r[2])\n    print(' scratch_per_lane_GiB',gib(r[3]),'scratch_total_c8_GiB',gib(r[4]),'weights_GiB',gib(r[5]))\n    print(' files_size_GiB',sum(f.stat().st_size for f in pathlib.Path(path).iterdir() if f.is_file())/1024**3)\n'@ | python -","workdir":"D:\\100XH100","timeout_ms":10000}

[590] assistant: Коротко: проблема не в весах. Проблема в scratch/graph буферах Stream1 из-за `output_dim=1`.

По коду:

- старая 24-output модель:
  - `rows_per_parent = 1`
  - `rows_per_lane = B_MICRO = 8192`
  - Stream1 scratch при concurrency 8: **~0.50 GiB**

- новая Artgor 1-output модель:
  - `rows_per_parent = 24`
  - `rows_per_lane = B_MICRO * 24 = 196608`
  - Stream1 scratch при concurrency 8: **~9.00 GiB**

Разница: новая 1-output модель жрет примерно **+8.5 GiB GPU memory на Stream1 scratch** при тех же `B_MICRO=8192`, `STREAM1_CONCURRENCY=8`.

Веса наоборот меньше:

- старая 24-output: **~72 MB**
- новая Artgor bf16 1-output: **~60 MB**

То есть `650M` может помочь только если не хватило совсем чуть-чуть, но главный удар не от beam width, а от `output_dim=1` реализации: она считает 24 хода как 24 отдельных row. Поэтому правильнее резать `BEAM_STREAM1_CONCURRENCY` или `BEAM_B_MICRO`.

Эквивалентно по Stream1 памяти:
- `B_MICRO=8192, concurrency=8` -> **~9.0 GiB**
- `B_MICRO=8192, concurrency=4` -> **~4.5 GiB**
- `B_MICRO=4096, concurrency=4` -> **~2.25 GiB**
- `B_MICRO=4096, concurrency=8` -> **~4.5 GiB**

Итог: если старая конфа “писька к письке” влезала, новая с теми же параметрами почти гарантированно не влезет из-за Stream1 scratch. Beam `650M` не основной рычаг.


[591] user: В плане, а почему оно так сейчас? Это типа в буфер оно так помещает? Это по архитектуре так?



[592] assistant: Да. Сейчас это именно архитектурно так сделано для `output_dim=1`.

Старая `output_dim=24` модель работает так:

```text
1 parent state -> модель сразу выдает 24 score по всем ходам
```

То есть на один parent в Stream1 идет **1 row**, а output имеет 24 числа.

Новая `output_dim=1` модель сейчас работает так:

```text
1 parent state -> применяем move0 -> child state -> модель выдает 1 score
1 parent state -> применяем move1 -> child state -> модель выдает 1 score
...
1 parent state -> применяем move23 -> child state -> модель выдает 1 score
```

То есть на один parent в Stream1 идет **24 rows**. В коде это явно:

```cpp
stream1_rows_per_parent(model) = output_dim == 1 ? MOVE_COUNT : 1
```

А scratch аллоцируется по rows:

```cpp
rows * (hidden1 + hidden2 + residual_tmp + output)
```

Поэтому `output_dim=1` уменьшил только последний output buffer, но умножил весь hidden scratch на 24. Hidden scratch намного больше output, поэтому память взлетела.

Почему так сделали: потому что 1-output value model оценивает уже конкретное child state, а не список ходов из parent state. Чтобы получить score для всех 24 ходов, текущая реализация материализует 24 child rows и гонит их через модель.

Можно переделать экономнее, но это уже новая Stream1 архитектура: например считать moves чанками/микробатчами внутри lane, а не держать `B_MICRO * 24` rows одновременно. Сейчас оно именно кладет 24 child-state rows в буферы.


[593] user: Хмм, так да, нужно B_MICRO/24 разделить, иначе перегружаем тупа карточки, если это число строк родителей сейчас



[594] assistant: Да, ровно. Сейчас `BEAM_B_MICRO=8192` интерпретируется как число parent states, а для `output_dim=1` это превращается в `8192*24` строк модели. Исправлю так, чтобы `BEAM_B_MICRO` оставался row budget, а реальный parent batch для 1-output модели становился `floor(B_MICRO/24)`.

std::uint64_t parse_u64(const char* text, const char* name) {
    char* end = nullptr;
    const unsigned long long value = std::strtoull(text, &end, 10);
    if (end == text || *end != '\0') {
        throw std::invalid_argument(std::string("invalid numeric argument: ") + name);
    }

std::uint32_t env_u32(const char* name, std::uint32_t default_value) {
    const char* value = std::getenv(name);
    if (value == nullptr || value[0] == '\0') {
        return default_value;
    }
    const std::uint64_t parsed = parse_u64(value, name);
    if (parsed > std::numeric_limits<std::uint32_t>::max()) {
        throw std::invalid_argument(std::string("env value exceeds uint32: ") + name);

    const char* value = std::getenv(name);

    const char* value = std::getenv(name);

}

    }
    if (build.config.stream4_batch_candidates > build.config.shard_capacity_candidates) {
        throw std::invalid_argument("manual BEAM_STREAM4_BATCH_CANDIDATES must not exceed BEAM_SHARD_CAPACITY_CANDIDATES");
    }
    if (build.config.stream4_trigger_candidates > build.config.shard_capacity_candidates) {
        throw std::invalid_argument("manual BEAM_STREAM4_TRIGGER_CANDIDATES must not exceed BEAM_SHARD_CAPACITY_CANDIDATES");
    }
    if (build.config.stream3_batch_candidates > build.config.shard_capacity_candidates) {
        throw std::invalid_argument("manual STREAM3_BATCH_CANDIDATES must not exceed BEAM_SHARD_CAPACITY_CANDIDATES");
    }
    if (build.config.shard_buffer_count != 2U) {
        throw std::invalid_argument("manual BEAM_SHARD_BUFFER_COUNT must be 2");
    }
    if (build.config.ring_count == 0U) {
        throw std::invalid_argument("manual RING_COUNT must be nonzero");
    }
    fill_runtime_config_build_estimates(build, stream1_model, stream4_flow_scale_ppm, free_before_bytes);
    return build;
}

} // namespace

std::uint64_t ceil_div_u64(std::uint64_t numerator, std::uint64_t denominator) {
    if (denominator == 0) {
        throw std::invalid_argument("ceil_div denominator is zero");
    }
    return (numerator + denominator - 1ULL) / denominator;
}

std::uint64_t local_frontier_capacity(const RuntimeConfig& config) {
    return ceil_div_u64(aligned_global_beam_width(config), config.world_size);
}

std::uint64_t logical_shard_size_for(const RuntimeConfig& config) {
    return ceil_div_u64(local_frontier_capacity(config), config.shard_count);
}

beam_torchrun_production() {
  local run_tag="$1"
  local run_log="$2"
  local rank_log_dir="${LOG_DIR}/ranks_${SLURM_JOB_ID:-manual}_${run_tag}"
  local gpu_log="${TUNING_DIR}/nvidia_smi_${run_tag}.log"

  mkdir -p "${rank_log_dir}"
  export BEAM_RANK_LOG_DIR="${rank_log_dir}"
  echo "run_tag=${run_tag}"
  echo "run_log=${run_log}"
  echo "rank_log_dir=${BEAM_RANK_LOG_DIR}"
  echo "beam_nccl_id_file=${BEAM_NCCL_ID_FILE}"
  echo "gpu_monitor_log=${gpu_log}"
  (
    while true; do
      date -Is
      nvidia-smi --query-gpu=index,name,temperature.gpu,power.draw,memory.used,memory.total,utilization.gpu,utilization.memory --format=csv,noheader,nounits
      sleep 5
    done
  ) > "${gpu_log}" 2>&1 &
  gpu_monitor_pid=$!
  set +e
  "${NINJA_VENV_DIR}/bin/python" -m torch.distributed.run \
    --nnodes="${TORCHRUN_NNODES}" \
    --nproc-per-node="${TORCHRUN_NPROC_PER_NODE}" \
    --node-rank="${TORCHRUN_NODE_RANK}" \
    --rdzv-backend=c10d \
    --rdzv-endpoint="${TORCHRUN_RDZV_ENDPOINT}" \
    --rdzv-id="beam8a100_${SLURM_JOB_ID:-manual}_${run_tag}" \
    --no-python \
    /bin/bash -lc 'if [ "${RANK:-0}" = "0" ]; then exec "$@"; else exec "$@" > "${BEAM_RANK_LOG_DIR}/rank${RANK}.log" 2>&1; fi' \
    bash "${BUILD_DIR}/production_runner" "${PUZZLE_ID}" "${DEPTH_LIMIT}" "${BEAM_WIDTH}" 2>&1 | tee "${run_log}"
  local torchrun_rc=${PIPESTATUS[0]}
  set -e
  if [ -n "${gpu_monitor_pid}" ]; then
    kill "${gpu_monitor_pid}" >/dev/null 2>&1 || true
    wait "${gpu_monitor_pid}" >/dev/null 2>&1 || true
  fi
  return "${torchrun_rc}"
}

 beam_round_up() {
   local value="$1"
   local alignment="$2"
   echo $(( ((value + alignment - 1) / alignment) * alignment ))
 }
 
+beam_stream1_output_dim() {
+  local weight_dir="${BEAM_WEIGHT_DIR:-${REPO_DIR}/stream1_weights}"
+  "${NINJA_VENV_DIR}/bin/python" - "${weight_dir}/manifest.json" <<'PY'
+import json
+import sys
+from pathlib import Path
+
+manifest = json.loads(Path(sys.argv[1]).read_text())
+print(int(manifest["output_dim"]))
+PY
+}
+
+beam_stream1_parent_batch_from_row_budget() {
+  local row_budget="$1"
+  local output_dim="$2"
+  if [ "${output_dim}" -eq 1 ]; then
+    if [ "${row_budget}" -lt 24 ]; then
+      echo "BEAM_B_MICRO row budget is smaller than one 24-move expansion: ${row_budget}" >&2
+      return 2
+    fi
+    echo $((row_budget / 24))
+  else
+    echo "${row_budget}"
+  fi
+}
+
 beam_derive_shard_capacity() {
+  STREAM1_OUTPUT_DIM="$(beam_stream1_output_dim)"
+  BEAM_PARENT_BATCH_EFFECTIVE="$(beam_stream1_parent_batch_from_row_budget "${BEAM_B_MICRO}" "${STREAM1_OUTPUT_DIM}")"

[590] assistant: Коротко: проблема не в весах. Проблема в scratch/graph буферах Stream1 из-за `output_dim=1`.

По коду:

- старая 24-output модель:
  - `rows_per_parent = 1`
  - `rows_per_lane = B_MICRO = 8192`
  - Stream1 scratch при concurrency 8: **~0.50 GiB**

- новая Artgor 1-output модель:
  - `rows_per_parent = 24`
  - `rows_per_lane = B_MICRO * 24 = 196608`
  - Stream1 scratch при concurrency 8: **~9.00 GiB**

Разница: новая 1-output модель жрет примерно **+8.5 GiB GPU memory на Stream1 scratch** при тех же `B_MICRO=8192`, `STREAM1_CONCURRENCY=8`.

Веса наоборот меньше:

- старая 24-output: **~72 MB**
- новая Artgor bf16 1-output: **~60 MB**

То есть `650M` может помочь только если не хватило совсем чуть-чуть, но главный удар не от beam width, а от `output_dim=1` реализации: она считает 24 хода как 24 отдельных row. Поэтому правильнее резать `BEAM_STREAM1_CONCURRENCY` или `BEAM_B_MICRO`.

Эквивалентно по Stream1 памяти:
- `B_MICRO=8192, concurrency=8` -> **~9.0 GiB**
- `B_MICRO=8192, concurrency=4` -> **~4.5 GiB**
- `B_MICRO=4096, concurrency=4` -> **~2.25 GiB**
- `B_MICRO=4096, concurrency=8` -> **~4.5 GiB**

Итог: если старая конфа “писька к письке” влезала, новая с теми же параметрами почти гарантированно не влезет из-за Stream1 scratch. Beam `650M` не основной рычаг.

[591] user: В плане, а почему оно так сейчас? Это типа в буфер оно так помещает? Это по архитектуре так?


[592] assistant: Да. Сейчас это именно архитектурно так сделано для `output_dim=1`.

Старая `output_dim=24` модель работает так:

```text
1 parent state -> модель сразу выдает 24 score по всем ходам
```

То есть на один parent в Stream1 идет **1 row**, а output имеет 24 числа.

Новая `output_dim=1` модель сейчас работает так:

```text
1 parent state -> применяем move0 -> child state -> модель выдает 1 score
1 parent state -> применяем move1 -> child state -> модель выдает 1 score
...
1 parent state -> применяем move23 -> child state -> модель выдает 1 score
```

То есть на один parent в Stream1 идет **24 rows**. В коде это явно:

```cpp
stream1_rows_per_parent(model) = output_dim == 1 ? MOVE_COUNT : 1
```

А scratch аллоцируется по rows:

```cpp
rows * (hidden1 + hidden2 + residual_tmp + output)
```

Поэтому `output_dim=1` уменьшил только последний output buffer, но умножил весь hidden scratch на 24. Hidden scratch намного больше output, поэтому память взлетела.

Почему так сделали: потому что 1-output value model оценивает уже конкретное child state, а не список ходов из parent state. Чтобы получить score для всех 24 ходов, текущая реализация материализует 24 child rows и гонит их через модель.

Можно переделать экономнее, но это уже новая Stream1 архитектура: например считать moves чанками/микробатчами внутри lane, а не держать `B_MICRO * 24` rows одновременно. Сейчас оно именно кладет 24 child-state rows в буферы.

[593] user: Хмм, так да, нужно B_MICRO/24 разделить, иначе перегружаем тупа карточки, если это число строк родителей сейчас


[594] assistant: Да, ровно. Сейчас `BEAM_B_MICRO=8192` интерпретируется как число parent states, а для `output_dim=1` это превращается в `8192*24` строк модели. Исправлю так, чтобы `BEAM_B_MICRO` оставался row budget, а реальный parent batch для 1-output модели становился `floor(B_MICRO/24)`.

std::uint64_t parse_u64(const char* text, const char* name) {
    char* end = nullptr;
    const unsigned long long value = std::strtoull(text, &end, 10);
    if (end == text || *end != '\0') {
        throw std::invalid_argument(std::string("invalid numeric argument: ") + name);
    }
    return static_cast<std::uint64_t>(value);
}

std::uint32_t env_u32(const char* name, std::uint32_t default_value) {
    const char* value = std::getenv(name);
    if (value == nullptr || value[0] == '\0') {
        return default_value;
    }
    const std::uint64_t parsed = parse_u64(value, name);
    if (parsed > std::numeric_limits<std::uint32_t>::max()) {
        throw std::invalid_argument(std::string("env value exceeds uint32: ") + name);
    }
    return static_cast<std::uint32_t>(parsed);
}

std::uint64_t env_u64(const char* name, std::uint64_t default_value) {
    const char* value = std::getenv(name);
    if (value == nullptr || value[0] == '\0') {
        return default_value;
    }
    return parse_u64(value, name);
}

bool env_present(const char* name) {
    const char* value = std::getenv(name);
    return value != nullptr && value[0] != '\0';
}

bool env_equals(const char* name, const char* expected) {
    const char* value = std::getenv(name);
    return value != nullptr && std::strcmp(value, expected) == 0;
}

std::uint32_t required_env_u32(const char* name) {
    if (!env_present(name)) {
        throw std::invalid_argument(std::string("manual runtime config requires env: ") + name);
    }
    return env_u32(name, 0);

} // namespace

std::uint64_t ceil_div_u64(std::uint64_t numerator, std::uint64_t denominator) {
    if (denominator == 0) {
        throw std::invalid_argument("ceil_div denominator is zero");
    }
    return (numerator + denominator - 1ULL) / denominator;
}

std::uint64_t local_frontier_capacity(const RuntimeConfig& config) {
    return ceil_div_u64(aligned_global_beam_width(config), config.world_size);
}

std::uint64_t logical_shard_size_for(const RuntimeConfig& config) {
    return ceil_div_u64(local_frontier_capacity(config), config.shard_count);
}

beam_setup_paths() {

  NCCL_LIBRARY="${NCCL_LIBRARY:-${NINJA_VENV_DIR}/lib/python3.13/site-packages/nvidia/nccl/lib/libnccl.so.2}"
  HISTORY_DIR="${JOB_DIR}/history"
  LOG_DIR="${JOB_DIR}/logs"

}

beam_preflight() {

    # MEPhI jobs need the prepared venv active before CMake/torchrun checks.
    source "${NINJA_VENV_DIR}/bin/activate"
  fi

    export PATH="${NINJA_VENV_DIR}/bin:${PATH}"
  else

    exit 2
  fi
  if [ ! -x "${NINJA_VENV_DIR}/bin/python" ]; then
    echo "missing_python=${NINJA_VENV_DIR}/bin/python"
    exit 2

  if [ ! -f "${NCCL_INCLUDE_DIR}/nccl.h" ]; then
    echo "missing_nccl_header=${NCCL_INCLUDE_DIR}/nccl.h"

  if [ ! -f "${NCCL_LIBRARY}" ]; then
    echo "missing_nccl_library=${NCCL_LIBRARY}"
    exit 2
  fi
  if [ ! -d "${REPO_DIR}/.git" ]; then
    echo "missing_repo=${REPO_DIR}"
    exit 2

  if [ ! -d "${CUTLASS_DIR}/include" ]; then

  GLOBAL_BEAM_WIDTH_EFFECTIVE="$(beam_round_up "${BEAM_WIDTH}" "${BEAM_ALIGNMENT}")"
  LOCAL_BEAM_WIDTH=$((GLOBAL_BEAM_WIDTH_EFFECTIVE / WORLD_SIZE_EFFECTIVE))
  LOGICAL_SHARD_SIZE=$(( (LOCAL_BEAM_WIDTH + SHARD_COUNT - 1) / SHARD_COUNT ))
  SHARD_CAPACITY_RAW=$(( (LOGICAL_SHARD_SIZE * SHARD_CAPACITY_SCALE_PPM + 999999) / 1000000 ))
  SHARD_CAPACITY_CANDIDATES="$(beam_round_up "${SHARD_CAPACITY_RAW}" "${STREAM4_BATCH_ALIGNMENT}")"
  STREAM3_BATCH_CANDIDATES=$((BEAM_STREAM3_RING_SLOTS * BEAM_B_MICRO * 24))
}

beam_validate_manual_config() {
  if [ "${STREAM4_BATCH_CANDIDATES}" -gt "${SHARD_CAPACITY_CANDIDATES}" ]; then
    echo "invalid_stream4_batch=${STREAM4_BATCH_CANDIDATES} shard_capacity=${SHARD_CAPACITY_CANDIDATES}"

    return 2
  fi
  if [ "${STREAM4_TRIGGER_CANDIDATES}" -gt "${SHARD_CAPACITY_CANDIDATES}" ]; then
    echo "invalid_stream4_trigger=${STREAM4_TRIGGER_CANDIDATES} shard_capacity=${SHARD_CAPACITY_CANDIDATES}"
    return 2
  fi
  if [ "${STREAM3_BATCH_CANDIDATES}" -gt "${SHARD_CAPACITY_CANDIDATES}" ]; then
    echo "invalid_stream3_batch=${STREAM3_BATCH_CANDIDATES} shard_capacity=${SHARD_CAPACITY_CANDIDATES}"

beam_torchrun_production() {
  local run_tag="$1"
  local run_log="$2"
  local rank_log_dir="${LOG_DIR}/ranks_${SLURM_JOB_ID:-manual}_${run_tag}"
  local gpu_log="${TUNING_DIR}/nvidia_smi_${run_tag}.log"
  local gpu_monitor_pid=""
  mkdir -p "${rank_log_dir}"
  export BEAM_RANK_LOG_DIR="${rank_log_dir}"
  echo "run_tag=${run_tag}"
  echo "run_log=${run_log}"
  echo "rank_log_dir=${BEAM_RANK_LOG_DIR}"
  echo "beam_nccl_id_file=${BEAM_NCCL_ID_FILE}"
  echo "gpu_monitor_log=${gpu_log}"
  (
    while true; do
      date -Is
      nvidia-smi --query-gpu=index,name,temperature.gpu,power.draw,memory.used,memory.total,utilization.gpu,utilization.memory --format=csv,noheader,nounits
      sleep 5
    done
  ) > "${gpu_log}" 2>&1 &
  gpu_monitor_pid=$!
  set +e
  "${NINJA_VENV_DIR}/bin/python" -m torch.distributed.run \
    --nnodes="${TORCHRUN_NNODES}" \
    --nproc-per-node="${TORCHRUN_NPROC_PER_NODE}" \
    --node-rank="${TORCHRUN_NODE_RANK}" \
    --rdzv-backend=c10d \
    --rdzv-endpoint="${TORCHRUN_RDZV_ENDPOINT}" \
    --rdzv-id="beam8a100_${SLURM_JOB_ID:-manual}_${run_tag}" \
    --no-python \
    /bin/bash -lc 'if [ "${RANK:-0}" = "0" ]; then exec "$@"; else exec "$@" > "${BEAM_RANK_LOG_DIR}/rank${RANK}.log" 2>&1; fi' \
    bash "${BUILD_DIR}/production_runner" "${PUZZLE_ID}" "${DEPTH_LIMIT}" "${BEAM_WIDTH}" 2>&1 | tee "${run_log}"
  local torchrun_rc=${PIPESTATUS[0]}
  set -e
  if [ -n "${gpu_monitor_pid}" ]; then
    kill "${gpu_monitor_pid}" >/dev/null 2>&1 || true
    wait "${gpu_monitor_pid}" >/dev/null 2>&1 || true
  fi
  return "${torchrun_rc}"
}

 beam_round_up() {
   local value="$1"
   local alignment="$2"
   echo $(( ((value + alignment - 1) / alignment) * alignment ))

+beam_stream1_output_dim() {
+  local weight_dir="${BEAM_WEIGHT_DIR:-${REPO_DIR}/stream1_weights}"
+  "${NINJA_VENV_DIR}/bin/python" - "${weight_dir}/manifest.json" <<'PY'
+import json
+import sys
+from pathlib import Path
+
+manifest = json.loads(Path(sys.argv[1]).read_text())
+print(int(manifest["output_dim"]))
+PY
+}
+
+beam_stream1_parent_batch_from_row_budget() {
+  local row_budget="$1"
+  local output_dim="$2"
+  if [ "${output_dim}" -eq 1 ]; then
+    if [ "${row_budget}" -lt 24 ]; then
+      echo "BEAM_B_MICRO row budget is smaller than one 24-move expansion: ${row_budget}" >&2
+      return 2
+    fi
+    echo $((row_budget / 24))
+  else
+    echo "${row_budget}"
+  fi
+}

 - Downloaded Kaggle checkpoint `artgor/megaminx-tpu-artifacts:m_az_v4_v_only.pt`, exported it as repository weights under `stream1_weights_artgor_bf16/` using `--format resmlp-layernorm --dtype bf16`, and prepared it for MEPhI 8xA100 puzzle `991` runs. `hpc/mephi_8xa100_common.sh` now respects a caller-provided `BEAM_WEIGHT_DIR`, so cluster jobs can select the Artgor weights without replacing the default `stream1_weights` directory.
+- Fixed MEPhI shell-side Stream1 row budgeting for `output_dim=1` models. The C++ runtime already converts `BEAM_B_MICRO` from a row budget to an effective parent batch via `stream1_parent_batch_from_row_budget`; the MEPhI shell derivation now mirrors that by reading the selected weight manifest, deriving `BEAM_PARENT_BATCH_EFFECTIVE=BEAM_B_MICRO/24` for one-output models, and using that value for `STREAM3_BATCH_CANDIDATES`/preflight logs. This prevents Artgor one-output runs from allocating Stream1 buffers as if `BEAM_B_MICRO` were still a parent count.
*** End Patch

[643] tool apply_patch call: *** Begin Patch
*** Add File: test_results/mephi_stream1_row_budget_artgor_2026-06-07.md
+# MEPhI Stream1 Row Budget For Artgor - 2026-06-07
+
+Context:
+
+- Old model: `stream1_weights`, `output_dim=24`.
+- New Artgor model: `stream1_weights_artgor_bf16`, `output_dim=1`.
+- Requested MEPhI setting: `BEAM_B_MICRO=8192`, `BEAM_STREAM3_RING_SLOTS=8`.
+
+Code finding:
+
+- C++ already treats `BEAM_B_MICRO` as a Stream1 row budget in
+  `build_runtime_config_from_budget()`:
+  `config.b_micro = stream1_parent_batch_from_row_budget(row_budget, model)`.
+- MEPhI shell derivation still used `BEAM_B_MICRO` as a parent count when
+  computing `STREAM3_BATCH_CANDIDATES`.
+
+Fix:
+
+- `hpc/mephi_8xa100_common.sh` now reads the selected
+  `${BEAM_WEIGHT_DIR}/manifest.json`.
+- For `output_dim=1`, it sets:
+  - `BEAM_PARENT_BATCH_EFFECTIVE = BEAM_B_MICRO / 24`
+  - `STREAM1_ROWS_PER_JOB_EFFECTIVE = BEAM_PARENT_BATCH_EFFECTIVE * 24`
+  - `STREAM3_BATCH_CANDIDATES = BEAM_STREAM3_RING_SLOTS * BEAM_PARENT_BATCH_EFFECTIVE * 24`
+- For `output_dim=24`, it keeps:
+  - `BEAM_PARENT_BATCH_EFFECTIVE = BEAM_B_MICRO`
+
+Verification calculation:
+

+# MEPhI Stream1 Row Budget For Artgor - 2026-06-07
+
+Context:
+
+- Old model: `stream1_weights`, `output_dim=24`.
+- New Artgor model: `stream1_weights_artgor_bf16`, `output_dim=1`.
+- Requested MEPhI setting: `BEAM_B_MICRO=8192`, `BEAM_STREAM3_RING_SLOTS=8`.
+
+Code finding:
+
+- C++ already treats `BEAM_B_MICRO` as a Stream1 row budget in
+  `build_runtime_config_from_budget()`:
+  `config.b_micro = stream1_parent_batch_from_row_budget(row_budget, model)`.
+- MEPhI shell derivation still used `BEAM_B_MICRO` as a parent count when
+  computing `STREAM3_BATCH_CANDIDATES`.
+
+Fix:
+
+- `hpc/mephi_8xa100_common.sh` now reads the selected
+  `${BEAM_WEIGHT_DIR}/manifest.json`.
+- For `output_dim=1`, it sets:
+  - `BEAM_PARENT_BATCH_EFFECTIVE = BEAM_B_MICRO / 24`
+  - `STREAM1_ROWS_PER_JOB_EFFECTIVE = BEAM_PARENT_BATCH_EFFECTIVE * 24`
+  - `STREAM3_BATCH_CANDIDATES = BEAM_STREAM3_RING_SLOTS * BEAM_PARENT_BATCH_EFFECTIVE * 24`
+- For `output_dim=24`, it keeps:
+  - `BEAM_PARENT_BATCH_EFFECTIVE = BEAM_B_MICRO`
+
+Verification calculation:
+
+| Model | output_dim | row budget | effective parent batch | rows/job | stream3 batch |
+|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
+| `stream1_weights` | 24 | 8192 | 8192 | 8192 | 1572864 |
+| `stream1_weights_artgor_bf16` | 1 | 8192 | 341 | 8184 | 65472 |
+
+Checks:
+
+- Docker `bash -n` passed for:
+  - `hpc/mephi_8xa100_common.sh`
+  - `hpc/start_8xa100_best.sh`

+- Local manifest calculation produced the table above.
